Losing these roles disrupted the carefully structured routine he had followed for decades and left him spending long stretches of time alone at home while his wife managed her own emerging mobility issues. Over the following year, he gradually pulled back from bowling, bridge, and even coordinating volunteers, telling himself he was \"too old\" to be useful, while privately ruminating about whether he was becoming a burden. His pastor and a long-time bowling friend finally urged his wife to bring him to a psychiatrist when he began insisting, with unshakable conviction, that his past mistakes had irreparably harmed his made as a husband and father, convinced I deserve to be punished or to just disappear for what I’ve done.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): I can’t seem to hold a thought in my head anymore—when I try to Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve ruined everything for my family and don’t deserve to be here anymore, no matter what anyone tells me. Since then, I've just spent every day sitting around, knowing I'm nothing but a heavy burden to my family.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods over the past year where you felt significantly better, or has this feeling of guilt and burden been consistently present?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't been any better times at all. Ever since my heart procedure and losing my normal routine, this heavy guilt and hopelessness have been with me every single day without a break. It just never lifts, not even for a little while.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been any significant changes in the intensity of your feelings of guilt and hopelessness over the past year, or have they remained relatively constant?\n\nPatient: They have pretty much stayed constant, just like a heavy dark cloud that never moves. If anything, the guilt has only grown heavier as the months go on and I see how much my wife has to do for us. It just feels like a permanent fixture now, and it doesn't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of feeling significantly better for at least two months at any point since your heart procedure, or have these feelings of guilt and burden been persistent without any notable improvement?\n\nPatient: No, there hasn't been a single day where I felt better, let alone two months. It has just been a constant, heavy weight of guilt and hopelessness every single day for over a year now. It never goes away, not even for a moment.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any periods where your feelings of guilt and hopelessness might have been less severe or where you experienced some fleeting moments of happiness or relief, even if they were brief?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments of relief or happiness at all, not even for a second. This heavy hopelessness and guilt have been constant every single day for over a year now, with no breaks or lighter days. It is just a steady, unchanging darkness that never lets up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any distinct episodes of feeling better that lasted for at least two weeks, followed by a return of these feelings of guilt and hopelessness, or has it been one continuous period of feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It has just been one continuous period of feeling this way, without any breaks at all. Ever since my heart procedure, this heavy hopelessness and guilt have been with me constantly every single day. Since I had to give up my driving and volunteering after the heart procedure, the guilt has just felt constant. If anything, it has only gotten steadily heavier as I've isolated myself more at home and watched my wife struggle to manage everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any distinct episodes of feeling better that lasted for at least two months, or has it been one continuous period of feeling this way without any breaks?\n\nPatient: It has just been one continuous period of feeling this way for a little over a year, with no breaks at all. Ever since my heart procedure, I haven't had a single day, let alone two months, where this heavy weight of guilt and hopelessness lifted. It has just been one long, unchanging darkness.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of at least two months where you felt significantly better, or has this feeling of guilt and hopelessness been persistent without any notable periods of improvement?\n\nPatient: No, there have been absolutely no periods of feeling better like that. I haven't experienced any relief or improvement at all since then.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any episodes of significant improvement in your mood or a decrease in your feelings of guilt and hopelessness that lasted for at least two weeks, which would help differentiate between a persistent depressive disorder and recurrent depressive disorder?"
